Let's think of scoring and optimization details.

Function score from sequence a (raw nonneg): n=len(a), c=conv via FFT, sum S. Score =2n*max(c)/S^2 if S>0.01 else inf. We can normalize to sum=1 in optimization; score=2n*max(c). Constraints nonnegative. Score scale invariant.

Use FFT convolution: For sequences length n, pad to size next power/fftfriendly ≥2n-1. Compute F=np.fft.rfft(a, N), c=np.fft.irfft(F*F, N)[:2n-1]. Real small negative clip to 0. This matches direct convolution within floating error. For internal score, use max(np.maximum(c,0)). Since sequence nonneg, errors tiny.

Optimization approaches:
1. Use nonlinear local optimization with projected gradient on normalized p (sum=1). Objective f(p)=max(c_t). Score=2n f. Since scale fixed, minimize f. But objective nonsmooth and nonconvex. We can use Nelder-Mead? Too slow for n thousands. Use subgradient/soft-max.

Soft-max smoothing: h_λ(p) = (1/λ) log(Σ_t exp(λ c_t - λ *?)). To approximate max and keep gradient stable, we can subtract c_max: logsumexp(λ c)/λ + c_max? Actually logsumexp(λ c_t)/λ ≈ max + (log K)/λ. Derivative wrt p_i = (1/S? no) sum_t w_t ∂c_t/∂p_i, where w_t=exp(λ c_t)/Σ exp. This is smooth if λ finite. We can minimize h_λ with projected gradient/FISTA. As λ increases, converge to max. Use adaptive λ (annealing). Since objective is smooth convex? h_λ is sum of exponentials of quadratic; not convex necessarily. But local.

Alternatively, minimize square-sum of active loads: L = Σ c_t^2? This smooth (self-averaging) might flatten distribution. Minimizing sum of squares of convolution coefficients subject to sum=1 may reduce max and perhaps get flat. It is quartic in p (quadratic in c) nonconvex. Gradient: ∂Σ c_t^2 /∂p_i = 2 Σ_t 2 c_t ∂c_t? =4 Σ_t c_t a_{t-i}. This is convolution of c_t with p. Can compute via FFT: grad = 4 * (convc * p) window. This is like gradient ascent on entropy? It tends to equalize loads: high c_t gets weight. It may find good flat distributions. It doesn't directly minimize max, but can be a good heuristic. Objective J=Σ c_t^2. For uniform p, J? ∑ c_t^2 = (1/n^2)∑ triangular^2 ~? For flat, J minimal 1/(2n-1). Minimizing J likely reduces max and is differentiable. We can optimize J via gradient descent with simplex projection. This avoids max active set. It may converge to a distribution that smooths coefficients, maybe close to optimal max. Since minimizing sum squares penalizes high peaks; could yield score below sqrt? Need test mental. For n=2, p=(x,1-x), c=[x^2,2x(1-x),(1-x)^2]. J=x^4+4x^2(1-x)^2+(1-x)^4. Uniform x=.5 J=.0625+.25+.0625=.375. Best max p=(2/3,1/3) c=[4/9,4/9,1/9], J=16/81+16/81+1/81=33/81=0.407, uniform better for J. But max score uniform 2, p=(2/3) score1.777. So minimizing J worsens max for n=2. It favors more uniform, not skew. But for large n, sqrt has c_t=1/S^2 for n coefficients and lower tail; J? Might be not optimal. Need maybe minimize max directly or use mixed objective: J + μ*(max)^2? Or use log-sum.

Minimizing entropy / flatness maybe not exactly max, but could be okay. However sqrt has flat left half; J maybe high due many active. A sequence that distributes coefficient variance minimizes J and might have max ~sqrt average. Need examine.

Another objective: minimize ℓ_p norm of c for p large. Use p=4,10, etc. We can use power-sum (Σ c_t^q) with q large -> min max. q=2 not enough; q=8/16 better. Compute gradient c^{q-1} * p via FFT. For q=2 gradient as above; for even q, smooth. This might be good to flatten but avoid overuniform? For n=2, q large approximates max; q=4? Could find p 2/3. For normalized sum=1, min L_q tends to max as q→∞. Use large q (e.g. q=16 or 32) to approximate max. Exponentials maybe easier. We can scale c to [0,1] and compute logsumexp for numerical stability.

def project_simplex(v): sorted desc, find rho such that v_j - thr >0. theta=sum(v[:rho]-rho)/rho; return np.clip(v-theta,0,None). Add small epsilon and renormalize.

Softmax objective:
Given p, compute c. For stability, we can compute weights for gradient using logsumexp with parameter lam. Since c small, lam maybe chosen as L = scale * n. But c_max varies. If c_max=0, avoid.
Implementation:
def softmax_c(c, lam):
    x=lam*c
    m=np.max(x)
    ex=np.exp(x-m)
    s=ex.sum()
    val=(m+np.log(s))/lam
    w=ex/s
    return val,w
The objective approximates max + log(size)/lam. Since additive constant log(2n-1)/lam small. Minimizing val close.

### Plan for final algorithm

Top-level functions:
1. `fft_pair(a, b, need=None?)` maybe for convolution/cross. We need:

3. `project_simplex(v)` for normalized p.
4. `make_sqrt(n)` raw central binomial maybe scaled? Use recurrence a_0=1; a_{k}=a_{k-1}*(2k-1)/(2k)? Since a_k = C(2k,k)/4^k. Check recurrence: a_k/a_{k-1}= (2k-1)/(2k). Yes. Could generate up to n; values down ~1/√(πk). Normalize to sum1.
5. `make_param(n, break_frac, beta, power_frac?, tail_frac?)`: Left power + right constant, or more general left power + right power? Let's implement a few families:
   - family0: sqrt prefix (beta=0.5, no tail)
   - family1: two-level: for i< b*n: w = (i+1)^(-beta) ; else w=tail_level (parameter maybe). Normalize. To allow optimizing tail ratio, set first part amplitude1, second amplitude r. Could represent p_i = base * ((i+1)^(-beta) for i<k, r for i>=k). Then normalize. One can also let first part a + b? For param search, sample beta in [0,0.49,1?], b in [0.1,0.9], r in [0.1,10]. However if r too large, right edge p_{n-1} huge? p last =r/sum; need edge last constraint? p_last^2 score if too large. The optimizer will avoid. Use log-uniform sampling.
   - family2: smooth blend: p_i = (1+exp((i-k)/scale)))? But family1 may suffice.
   - family3: power with exponent on all n but add edge low mass? maybe not.
6. `local_optimize(p, n, time_budget, rng, target?)`:
   - We'll use smooth log-sum objective with projected multiplicative or Euclidean steps.
   - We need robust step. Let's design using scipy? Could use `scipy.optimize` for low-dimensional parameters but for full p use custom.

#### Full sequence optimization details

We can normalize p sum=1. Store p as float array. Nfft = next power of two >=3n-2 for gradient and c.

Softmax objective F(p; lam):
- Compute c via self conv full length 2n-1 (from FFT Nfft). Use `c = np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp, Nfft)[:2n-1]`. Clip negative to0.
- If lam maybe 0? use pct: weights proportional to exp((c - max)/tau) with tau smoothing in coefficient units. Since c ~ M/n, tau can be a fraction of max, e.g. tau = smooth_frac * max_c (like 0.05,0.01). Then weights w_t = exp((c_t - max)/tau) normalized. Objective F = max + tau * log(mean exp(...)). This approximates max and is numerically stable. This avoids λ*n. Use `tau` annealed from e.g. 0.1*max to 0.01*max. If max zero, no.
- Gradient weights w. Need h_i = ∑ w_t p_{t-i}. Use W_rev = w[::-1]; conv_p_w via FFT: Fp computed, Fw = rfft(W_rev padded Nfft). full = irfft(Fp * Fw, Nfft). h = full[n-1:2n-1][::-1]. Grad=2*h. Note w sum=1, ∑_i h_i = ∑_t w_t ∑_i p_{t-i}=S=1. So grad sum=2; constant 2/n. Fine.

Line search for multiplicative update:
Option A: Euclidean projected gradient:
   candidate = project_simplex(p - step * grad)
   step initial maybe 0.5? But grad components O? Let's estimate for normalized p. If active max, h_i ~ average? For uniform, grad constant2/n? h_i=1/n? grad=2/n. Step=10 gives change ~20/n; for n=1000 0.02. Maybe okay. Need adapt.
   Backtracking: for step in [init, init/2,...], if fast_score(candidate) < current_score - tol or F smoother < ... reduce. But evaluating F via FFT heavy. Limit line search maybe 3.
   Use init step based on gradient norm: For functions on simplex with Lipschitz L, optimal step=1/L. Estimate L ≈ n * max_c? Wait each c_t is quadratic with Hessian entries 2p, norm O? The max Hessian maybe O(sqrt n)? Let's derive: For smooth weighted sum of c_t, Hessian=4∑ w_t A_t A_t^T? A_t anti-diagonal permutation (Hadamard?) Norm ≤2√(∑w_t)=2. Actually ∂c_t/∂p =2A_t p, Hessian=2A_t? A_t is 0-1 anti-diagonal, spectral norm1. Weighted sum Hessian=4∑w_t A_t? Its spectral norm ≤4. Wait c_t = p^T A_t p, Hessian=2A_t (since A_t symmetric 0-1 anti-diag). The active gradient=2A_t p. The Hessian of weighted sum =4∑w_t A_t. A_t are nilpotent? Norm1. So Lipschitz O(1)! Is that true? ### Parametric family and coordinate optimization

`score_two_part(n, b, beta, r)` generates p and fast_score. For speed, generate arrays with vectorization:
```
k=int(b*n)
x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
vals=np.empty(n)
if beta==0: vals[:k]=1.0
else: vals[:k]=np.power(x[:k], -beta)  # if x>0; beta=0.5 okay
vals[k:]=r
# Need ensure no inf/nan; x[0]=1/(2n), beta .5 -> sqrt(2n) finite.
vals=np.clip(vals,0,None)
s=vals.sum()
if s<=0: return inf
p=vals/s
return fast_score(p)
```
Multiplication by irrational? fine.

```
def local_optimize(p, n, budget_s, rng, verbose=True):
    p = np.asarray(p,float).copy(); s=p.sum(); if s<=0: return
    p/=s
    N=1; while N<3*n-2: N*=2
    current_score = fast_score(p,N)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=current_score
    start=time.monotonic()
    it=0
    lambda_factor=20.0  # lambda = n * factor? We'll compute lambda = n*factor / max? Actually lambda maxc; use lambda = n*factor
    tau? Use lambda_maxc approach: lam = lambda_factor * n  (since maxc~K/n, lam*max~lambda_factor*K). For K~0.75, lam_max~15 if factor20.
    while time<deadline:
       c, weights = softmax_weights(p, lam, N?)
       h = weighted_h(p, weights, N)
       grad = 2*h
       grad -= grad.mean()
       for step in [step0, step0*0.5, step0*0.25, step0*0.1]:
          cand=project_simplex(p - step*grad)
          score_c=fast_score(cand,N)
          if score_c < best_score - 1e-12:
             p=cand; best...; break
       if no improvement:
          # try random perturbation or reduce lambda
       it+=1; adjust lambda_factor *=1.05? 
```

Function `smooth_grad_score(p,N, lam)`:
```
Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
c_full=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,N)
c=c_full[:2*n-1]
np.maximum(c,c,out=c)
maxc=c.max()
score=2*n*maxc
if maxc<=0: return score,c,None
x=lam*c
# robust: if lam*maxc very large, weights only max; use m
m=np.max(x)
ex=np.exp(x-m)
s=ex.sum()
w=ex/s
return score,c,w
```

### Improve full optimization using load equalization

Instead of generic projected gradient, we can directly minimize the maximum coefficient by active set linearization with variables in log-space? Maybe use the following iterative "power method" from convex feasibility (though nonconvex):
At each iteration, identify set A of coefficients near max. Construct a target vector h_i = ∑_{t∈A} w_t p_{t-i} (as above). Update p_new_i ∝ p_i / (1 + η * α_i), where α_i = (h_i / p_i - mean?)? This is multiplicative updates for log objective. Let's derive: We want reduce high coefficients; if p_i appears in high coefficients relative to its contribution, reduce it. Gradient of log-sum w.r.t log p_i: ∂F/∂log p_i = p_i * ∂F/∂p_i = 2 p_i h_i. Multiplicative gradient descent in log-p: p_i ← p_i exp(-η p_i h_i). Normalize. The exponent η p_i h_i. Since p_i h_i is contribution of variable i to weighted loads. If variable contributes a lot to max loads, reduce it. This is scale-invariant and relative. For uniform, p_i h_i constant? h_i=1/n? p_i=1/n => p_i h_i=1/n^2? constant, no change. For skew, can move. This avoids Euclidean physical units. Let's use this!

Update raw q_i = p_i * exp(-η * y_i), where y_i = p_i * h_i (or normalized y_i - mean? A constant shift only affects normalization, so subtract mean: y_i - mean(y)). Then normalize. η initial? y_i magnitude: For uniform p=1/n, h_i≈1/n, y=1/n^2 ~2e-5 for n4096. Need η~n^2 to move. That's huge. We can normalize y by current p? Maybe use y_i = h_i / p_i (relative gradient per log p): ∂F/∂log p / p? Actually ∂F/∂log p_i = 2 p_i h_i. For update p ∝ exp(-η ∂F/∂log p), exponent ~η p_i h_i. To get meaningful relative change, η should scale ~n^2. We can instead use gradient of F w.r.t log p divided by mean p_i h_i. Let y = p_i h_i; scale by its max. Then update exponent = -η * (y - mean(y)) / y_scale, with η~1-10. This is equivalent to multiplicative with relative contribution. Which variables have high y? If p_i large and involved in max loads, reduce.

Let's compute y_i = p_i * h_i. Since h_i sum1, p_i^2 h? For uniform, y_i=1/n^2, y_scale~1/n^2. For sqrt p_i~n^{-1/2} at edge, h_i? edge h maybe? y_scale ~? Use normalization by y_scale ensures steps O(η).

Update:
```
y = p*h
ys = max(abs(y - y.mean())) + 1e-12
exponent = -step * (y - y.mean()) / ys
q = p * exp(np.clip(exponent, -maxexp, maxexp))
p = q / q.sum()
```

```
def local_optimize_mult(p0, n, budget_s, rng, verbose):
    p = normalize(p0)
    N = nextpow2(3*n-2)
    best = p.copy(); score0 = fast_score(p,N)
    best_score=score0
    step = 2.0
    no_improve=0
    smoothing = 30.0  # target logsum exp at max
    update_count=0
    deadline=time...
    while now<deadline:
        score, c, w = softmax_score_weights(p, N, smoothing)
        if c is None: break
        h = weighted_h(...)
        y = p*h
        ymean = y.mean()
        ys = np.max(np.abs(y-ymean))
        if ys <= 1e-15: break
        improved=False
        for mult in [step, step*0.5, step*0.25, step*0.125]:
            expo = -mult*(y-ymean)/ys
            expo = np.clip(expo, -3.0, 3.0)
            q = p*np.exp(expo)
            # floor maybe
            if update_count % 10 ==0: q = (1-1e-8)*q + 1e-8/n? But O(n)
            s = q.sum()
            if s>0: q/=s
            new_score = fast_score(q,N)
            if new_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                p=q; best=q.copy(); best_score=new_score; improved=True; update_count+=1
                # maybe increase step slightly
                step = min(10.0, step*1.05)
                no_improve=0
                break
        if not improved:
            no_improve+=1
            step=max(0.1, step*0.9)
            if no_improve==50:
                # random perturbation
                scale = np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2, n))
                q=p*scale; q=np.maximum(q,0); s=q.sum(); q/=s

                if new_score < best_score:
                    p=q; best...; no_improve=0
                else:
                    no_improve=0
            if no_improve > 300: break

        if update_count % 50 ==0: smoothing *=1.1 (more focused?) Actually increase smoothing -> more concentrated weights; could help fine but may stuck. Use oscillate.
    return best, best_score
```

Need compute `softmax_score_weights` efficiently:
```
def softmax_score_weights(p,N,smooth):
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,N)
    L=2*n-1? Need n global? pass L. We'll pass length n.
    c=c[:L]
    np.maximum(c,c,out=c)
    maxc=float(c.max())
    score=2.0*n*maxc
    if maxc <= 0 or smooth <=0: return score,c,None
    tau=maxc/smooth
    x=(c-maxc)/tau
    # x <=0
    ex=np.exp(x)  # max at active=1, may underflow fine
    s=ex.sum()
    if s<=0: w=None
    else: w=ex/s
    return score,c,w
```
Because x≤0, exp stable no need subtract. smooth=20 => tau=max/20, weights for c=max-0.1max get exp(-2)=0.135. Broad enough. If smooth=100, only near max. Good.

```
          beta_prop = clip(beta + rng.normal(0,0.05),0,0.5)

          else count_fail++
          if count_fail>50: reset propose larger
          if time budget break
    return best p
```

```
def make_profile(n, b, beta, r, decay):
    k=int(b*n)
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    vals=np.empty(n)
    if beta==0: vals[:k]=1.0 else vals[:k]=x[:k]**(-beta)
    if decay>1e-9:
       tail = vals[-1]? break value at k-1? use start = vals[k-1] if k>0 else 1.0
       t=(np.arange(k,n))/n  # 0..1-b
       vals[k:] = r * start * np.exp(-decay * t)
    else:
       vals[k:] = r * (vals[k-1] if k>0 else 1.0) # or r? 
```

`make_profile` top-level:
```
def make_profile(n, b, beta, r, gamma):
    k=int(round(b*n)); k=max(1,min(n-1,k))
    idx=np.arange(n,dtype=float)+0.5
    x=idx/n
    vals=np.empty(n,float)
    if beta<=1e-12: vals[:k]=1.0
    else: vals[:k]=np.power(x[:k], -beta)
    start = vals[k-1]
    if k<n:
       if gamma <=1e-8:
           vals[k:] = r*start
       else:
           t = idx[k:]/n # or (idx[k:]-idx[k])/idx[k]
           vals[k:] = r*start * np.exp(-gamma * (t / max(1e-9, 1.0 - idx[k]/n)))
    s=vals.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return None
    return vals/s
```
Using t normalized by tail length so gamma independent.

Parameter search 4D: random sampling maybe 500. Use top 10 coordinate.
Coordinate refinement for 4D can do random perturbations all params.
For deterministic, include gamma=0 (two-part), sqrt (b=1? but k limited, beta=0.5,r=1,gamma=0? That's constant full? no). Sqrt profile: if beta=0.5 and gamma large to zero? Pure sqrt full length approx beta=0.5,gamma very large? For all n power no tail; we can include direct sqrt separately.
Also include beta=0.5,b=1? not allowed k=n? The full power beta=0.5 is sqrt family; make_profile with b=1 not good since k=n start. We can just add sqrt.

```
def local_optimize_mult(p0, n, budget_s, rng):
    p = normalize(p0)
    N = max_power(2*n-1)
    inv2n? 
    best=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p,N)
    step=1.0
    smooth=20.0
    fail=0
    start_time=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start_time+budget_s
    it=0
    while time<deadline:
       score,c,w = softmax_score_weights(n,p,N,smooth)
       if w is None: break

       y = p*h

       for s in [step, step*0.5, step*0.25, step*0.125]:
           expo = -s*(y-ymean)/yscale
           expo = np.clip(expo, -3.0, 3.0)
           q = p * np.exp(expo)

              qsum=q.sum(); q/=qsum

           if ns < best_score - 1e-12:
               p=q; best=q.copy(); best_score=ns; improved=True; fail=0
               if np.isfinite(ns) and ns < target? maybe continue
               step = min(5.0, step*1.02)
               break

           step = max(0.05, step*0.85)

           # sometimes anneal smooth
           if it % 50 == 0:
               if smooth < 300: smooth *=1.1
           if it % 200 == 0:
```

### Potential bugs and edge cases

- FFT convolution with p sum=1; c sum should 1. Floating errors can make c negative tiny. Use `np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)`.
- For very small maxc, tau=0; handle.
- `max_power`: `N=1; while N < 3*n-2: N <<=1`. For n=8192, N=32768. Good.
- Slice for h: `h = full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]`; length? 2n-1 - (n-1) = n. Good indices n-1 through 2n-2 inclusive. For n=1? sequence length at least maybe >1; if n=1, 2n-1=1, slice [0:1] okay. But n large.
- In `weighted_h`, W_rev length 2n-1: `w_rev = w[::-1]` copies. Create `w_rev = np.empty(2*n-1); w_rev[:] = w[::-1]` maybe.
- Need pad to N. `np.fft.rfft(w_rev, N)` automatically zero pads.
- In `softmax_score_weights`, c length L=2n-1; x=(c-maxc)/tau length L. w length L. For smooth large, exp underflows to0 for many, sum at least number max (maybe 1). okay.
- In `local_optimize_mult`, y = p*h. p length n, h length n. h nonnegative. If p has zeros, q zeros remain (unless floor). Add floor every iteration? If q = q + 1e-12, zeros become small; normalization slightly. This may increase active support and help. But if n large, floor 1e-12 negligible; after many iterations may accumulate to ~1e-8. Use floor=1e-10 maybe. But affects score minimally. We can add `q += 1e-12` and normalize; okay.
- Fast_score for candidate: If sum after floor zero? no.
- External evaluator clips negative: our p nonnegative.

`profile_from_params(n,b,beta,r,gamma)`: return p or None. Need handle b values: if b<=0.01 or >=0.99. Use k=int(round(b*n)); k=min(max(k,1),n-1). If n small.

Vectorized generation:
```
def make_profile(n,b,beta,r,gamma):
    if n<=0: return None
    k=int(round(b*n))
    if k<1 or k>=n: k=max(1,min(n-1,k))
    idx=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
    x=idx/n # maybe start 0; avoid x=0 by using idx+0.5
    x=idx/n + 1.0/(2.0*n)
    vals=np.empty(n,dtype=np.float64)
    if beta <= 1e-8:
       vals[:k]=1.0
    else:
       vals[:k]=np.power(x[:k], -beta)
    start=vals[k-1]
    if k<n:
       tail_len=n-k
       t=(idx[k:] - idx[k]) / float(tail_len)
       if gamma <=1e-8:
          vals[k:]=r*start
       else:
          vals[k:]=r*start*np.exp(-gamma*t)
    s=vals.sum()
    if not finite or s<=0: return None
    return vals/s
```
For beta near0.5, x[0]=0.5/n; x^-beta finite. Good. If gamma huge, exp underflow tail to0; okay. If r huge/small, sum finite. Clip `vals=np.clip(vals,0,1e100)` maybe.

`make_sqrt(n)`:
```
a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
for i in range(1,n): a[i]=a[i-1]*(2.0*i-1.0)/(2.0*i)
s=a.sum(); return a/s
```
For n large loop O(n) okay. Could vectorize recurrence with np.cumprod:
```
ratios = (2*np.arange(1,n)-1)/(2*np.arange(1,n))
a = np.concatenate(([1.0], np.cumprod(ratios)))
```
Use if n>1.

### `_fast_score_N` details

```
def _fast_score_N(a, N):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 0.01? Internal can normalize any; external requires >0.01 but scale invariant, so if s<=0 return inf. Use if s<=0: inf.
    p=a/s
    F=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    np.maximum(c,c,out=c)
    mx=c.max()
    return float(2.0*n*mx)
```
If `N < 2n-1`, convolution wraps; ensure N passed sufficiently. For `_fast_score` compute N = _next_power(2*n-1). Use Np maybe power of two. For local, N3 maybe used for score and safe. If score with N3 and exact, okay.

If a raw values not normalized and sum huge, normalization okay.

### More about local update and smoothing

Let's define `_smooth_weights(c, smooth)`:
```
if smooth <=0: return None
maxc=c.max()
if maxc <=0: return None
tau=maxc/smooth
# x = (c - maxc) / tau ; since smooth maybe large, tau small
x = (c - maxc) * (smooth / maxc)
ex = np.exp(np.minimum(x,0.0))  # x <=0
s = ex.sum()
if s<=0: return None
return ex/s
```
If smooth very large, tau tiny; x for c=max-epsilon/tau large negative. At least active max ex=1. Good.

`_smooth_score_weights` returns score (using hard max) and weights. It computes c. In local, we also might use a smooth objective for acceptance? We use actual score.

Line search: after candidate, score = hard max. It may improve even if smooth not. Good.

We can implement in local reset: with some probability, blend current p with a random sparse profile: q = (1-α)p + α*sparse_profile, normalize. This can explore new basins. Good.

Generate `make_sparse_random(n, m, rng)`? If m~n/2 random positive? But random uniform score2. Maybe use random exponential weights? Could add.

In local fail, instead of multiplicative small, occasionally (e.g., every 50 fails) do a larger hybrid with a random profile from `_make_profile` with random params or random Fourier. Since we can call make profile. This can jump basin. Implement in local? Avoid too complex. In `_local_optimize_mult`, when fail count high, choose:
- 50%: multiplicative random p
- 50%: random param profile with random b,beta,r,gamma
```
if fail==50 or fail==150:
   b=rng.uniform(.1,.9); beta=rng.uniform(0,.5); r=exp(rng.uniform(-2,2)); gamma=rng.choice([0, rng.uniform(0,20)]); p2=make_profile(...)
   blend = (1-rand_mix)*p + rand_mix*p2 normalize; evaluate if improves best? But if not improve best, maybe set p=blend to explore? We can set p=blend even if score worse than best, because currently p is best? We should not sacrifice best. But for basin jump, allow p to a worse candidate if no progress; keep best. We can set p=blend, no_improve=0, step reset. This is simulated annealing: accept worse candidates in p only for exploration, but keep global best. If it leads to better, great. Score may temporarily high; local will climb back. Good.
```
However if p becomes bad, gradient may be unstable but okay. Need keep best.

Accepting worse: after fail==80, do `q = blend(p, random_profile, mix=0.3)` and set p=q. This broadens search. Limit time.

### Local best vs current score maintain

In `_local_optimize_mult`, initialize `best=p`, `best_score`. `p` can be current exploration. At end return best. If we set p to worse, best still best. Need maybe if p score much worse and no improvement for more fails, blend again.

Line search accepts only if improves best, not current. If p is worse, to climb back, we need accept improvements over current, not necessarily best? Suppose p worse score 1.8, best1.57; line search looking for <best_score will not accept improvements to 1.75, so cannot climb back. Better: For current step, accept if improves *current score* or best? We can maintain `current_score` and accept if candidate score < current_score - tol, updating current p; and if candidate improves best, update best. This allows escape. So in local loop:
- Compute current score from p? We have score from `_smooth_score_weights` (hard max of current p) at start. Use `cur_score=score`.
- Candidate accept if `ns < cur_score - 1e-12`; update p and cur_score. If `ns < best_score`, update best.
- If no candidate improves current, fail.
This is standard local descent from current, even if current worse than best. Good.

Thus algorithm:
```
cur_score = score from weights function
while ...:
   score, c,w = ... # score=cur_score
   improved=False
   for step ...:
      q=...
      ns=score(q)
      if ns < cur_score - tol:
          p=q; cur_score=ns; improved=True
          if ns < best_score: best=q; best_score=ns
          break
   if not improved: ... fail
```
This is better.

When exploring blend to worse p, update cur_score=score(blend), best unchanged.

If cur_score very high, gradient may have active max high, step line search may improve. Good.

`_h_from_weighted`:
```
def _h_from_weighted(n,p,w,N):
    wrev = w[::-1].copy()
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    Fw=np.fft.rfft(wrev,N)
    full=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fw,N)
    h=full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    # numerical tiny clamp
    np.maximum(h,h,out=h)
    return h
```
This recompute Fp; in main we already computed Fp in smooth_score. To save, we can combine: `_score_weights` returns Fp maybe. But Fp an array length N//2+1; memory okay. Let's optimize:
```
def _score_weights_Fp(n,p,N,smooth):
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,N)
    ...
    return score,c,w,Fp
```
Then `_h_from_Fp_w(n,w,Fp,N)` uses Fp*Fw. This saves one FFT. Implement.

### `_score_weights_Fp` robust

```
def _score_weights_Fp(n,p,N,smooth):
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    cc=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,N)
    L=2*n-1
    c=cc[:L]
    # c might have small imaginary? irfft real.
    np.maximum(c,c,out=c)
    maxc=float(c.max())
    score=2.0*n*maxc
    if maxc <= 0.0 or smooth <=0.0:
       return score,c,None,Fp
    tau=maxc/smooth
    # x = (c - maxc)/tau = (c - maxc)*smooth/maxc
    x = (c - maxc) * (smooth / maxc)
    # x <= 0 but due round may positive tiny; clip
    np.minimum(x,0.0,out=x)
    ex=np.exp(x)
    s=ex.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite:
       return score,c,None,Fp
    w=ex/s
    return score,c,w,Fp
```
If smooth is huge e.g. 1000, x for max=0, for others negative large; ex sum ~1. Good.

### `_make_random_fourier`

```
def _make_random_fourier(n, amp, rng):
    base=np.ones(n)
    # ensure sum not zero after projection
    for k in range(20):
       freq = int(rng.integers(1, max(2,n//2)))
       phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2*np.pi))
       coeff = float(rng.uniform(-amp, amp)/(k+1))
       t = (2*np.pi*freq*np.arange(n))/n
       base += coeff*np.cos(t+phase)
    p=np.clip(base,0,None)
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: p=np.ones(n)
    return p/s
```
Amp maybe 0.6. This can create shapes.

`_pow2(k)`:
```
def _pow2(k):
    if k <=1: return 1
    n=1
    while n < k:
        n <<=1
    return n
```

```
def _normalize_seq(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <=0: return None
    return a/s
```

```
def _local_optimize_mult(p0,n,budget_s,rng):
    p=np.asarray(p0,float).copy()
    s=p.sum(); if s<=0: return p0.copy(), inf
    p/=s
    N=_pow2(3*n-2)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=_score_normalized_N(p,N,n)
    cur_score=best_score
    step=1.0; smooth=20.0; fail=0; it=0
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s
    while time<deadline:
       score,c,w,Fp = _score_weights_Fp(n,p,N,smooth)
       if w is None: break
       # optional if score much worse than best and fail high, maybe random profile
       cur_score=score
       h=_h_from_Fp_w(n,w,Fp,N)
       y=p*h
       ym=float(y.mean())
       diff=y-ym
       ys=float(np.max(np.abs(diff)))
       if ys <=1e-15:
           # all same, try perturb
           fail +=1
       else:
           improved=False
           for tr in (step, step*0.5, step*0.25, step*0.125):
               expo=-tr*(diff/ys)
               np.clip(expo,-3.0,3.0,out=expo)
               q=p*np.exp(expo)
               q += 1e-12
               qs=float(q.sum())
               if qs>0: q/=qs
               ns=_score_normalized_N(q,N,n)
               if ns < cur_score - 1e-12:
                   p=q; cur_score=ns; improved=True
                   if ns < best_score:
                       best=q.copy(); best_score=ns
                   step = min(8.0, tr*1.05)
                   break
           if not improved:
               fail +=1
               step=max(0.05, step*0.9)
       if fail == 30 or fail == 80 or fail == 150:
           # exploration
           if fail < 80:
              sigma=0.15 + 0.05*(fail//10)
              mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
              q = p*mult
              qs=q.sum(); q/=qs
           else:
              # blend with random profile
              b=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.9)); beta=float(rng.uniform(0.0,0.5)); r=float(exp(rng.uniform(-2,2))); gamma=0.0 if rng.random()<0.3 else float(exp(rng.uniform(-1,2)))
              p2=_make_profile(n,b,beta,r,gamma)
              mix=float(rng.uniform(0.2,0.6))
              p2n = p2 if p2 is not None and p2.sum()>0 else p
              q=(1-mix)*p + mix*p2n
              q=np.clip(q,0,None); qs=q.sum()
              if qs>0: q/=qs
           qscore=_score_normalized_N(q,N,n)
           p=q; cur_score=qscore
           if qscore < best_score: best=q.copy(); best_score=qscore
           step=1.5; smooth=20.0; fail=0
       if improved? need var defined; if ys skip? set improved flag in outer.
```

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    total_deadline=time.monotonic()+max(0.0,float(budget_s))-30.0

    if hs is not None:

       if 128 <= arr.size <= 10000: # if not too large
          prep=_normalize_seq(arr)
          if prep is not None:
             N=_pow2(2*prep.size-1)
             sc=_score_normalized_N(prep,N,prep.size)
             if np.isfinite(sc):
                best_seq=prep; best_score=sc; best_n=prep.size
    # If no best, set sqrt 2048 as fallback maybe later.
    # Lengths

    # To decide budgets, use time remaining.
    def? no nested. We'll write loop over tuples.
    lengths = ( (2048, 240), (4096, 900) )
    for n, alloc in lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > total_deadline - 60: break
```

```
def _score_normalized_N(p,N,n):
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    cc=np.fft.irfft(Fp*Fp,N)
    L=2*n-1
    c=cc[:L]
    # If FFT numerical negatives, max still nonneg; but if all negative? max negative. Clamp.
    if c.size and c.max() <0: c.fill(0.0)
    else: np.maximum(c,c,out=c)
    return float(2.0*n*max(0.0, c.max()))
```

### Let's write final.
</think>

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative step-function heights minimizing

    score(a) = 2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))^2

using FFT-based convolution, explicit low-peak profile constructions
(edge-power / tail families, random Fourier starts), and a load-balanced
multiplicative local refinement.  The best sequence found before the time
budget is returned.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math


def _pow2(k):
    if k <= 1:
        return 1
    n = 1
    while n < k:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def _normalize_seq(a):
    try:
        x = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return x / s


def _score_normalized_N(p, N, n):
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    cc = np.fft.irfft(Fp * Fp, N)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    c = cc[:L]
    np.maximum(c, c, out=c)
    mx = float(c.max())
    if mx < 0.0:
        mx = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mx)


def _score_N(a, N):
    p = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return float('inf')
    p = p / s
    return _score_normalized_N(p, N, p.size)


def _score_seq(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    N = _pow2(2 * n - 1)
    return _score_N(a, N)


def _make_sqrt(n):
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    if n == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    i = np.arange(1, n, dtype=np.float64)
    ratios = (2.0 * i - 1.0) / (2.0 * i)
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    a[1:] = np.cumprod(ratios)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return a / s


def _make_profile(n, b, beta, r, gamma):
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    b = min(max(float(b), 0.02), 0.98)
    beta = min(max(float(beta), 0.0), 0.5)
    r = min(max(float(r), 1e-4), 1e4)
    gamma = min(max(float(gamma), 0.0), 100.0)

    k = int(round(b * n))
    if k < 1:
        k = 1
    if k >= n:
        k = n - 1

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    x = idx / float(n) + 1.0 / (2.0 * float(n))
    vals = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if beta <= 1e-10:
        vals[:k] = 1.0
    else:
        vals[:k] = np.power(x[:k], -beta)

    start = float(vals[k - 1])
    if k < n:
        tail_len = float(n - k)
        t = (idx[k:] - float(idx[k])) / tail_len
        if gamma <= 1e-10:
            vals[k:] = r * start
        else:
            vals[k:] = r * start * np.exp(-gamma * t)

    np.maximum(vals, vals, out=vals)
    s = float(vals.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return vals / s


def _profile_score_N(n, b, beta, r, gamma, N):
    p = _make_profile(n, b, beta, r, gamma)
    if p is None:
        return float('inf')
    return _score_normalized_N(p, N, n)


def _search_profile(n, budget_s, rng):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    Nscore = _pow2(2 * n - 1)
    candidates = []

    b_choices = (0.20, 0.30, 0.40, 0.50, 0.60, 0.70, 0.80, 0.90)
    beta_choices = (0.0, 0.12, 0.25, 0.35, 0.45, 0.49, 0.50)
    r_choices = (0.15, 0.35, 0.70, 1.00, 1.50, 2.50, 5.00)
    gam_choices = (0.0, 0.5, 1.5, 4.0, 10.0)

    for b in b_choices:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        for beta in beta_choices:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            for r in r_choices:
                for gam in gam_choices:
                    sc = _profile_score_N(n, b, beta, r, gam, Nscore)
                    if math.isfinite(sc):
                        candidates.append((sc, b, beta, r, gam))
                    if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                        break

    random_count = 0
    while random_count < 1400 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        random_count += 1
        b = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.95))
        beta = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.5))
        r = math.exp(float(rng.uniform(-2.5, 2.5)))
        if rng.random() < 0.35:
            gam = 0.0
        else:
            gam = math.exp(float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 3.0)))
        sc = _profile_score_N(n, b, beta, r, gam, Nscore)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            candidates.append((sc, b, beta, r, gam))

    if not candidates:
        return None, float('inf')

    candidates.sort()
    top_count = min(3, len(candidates))

    for idx in range(top_count):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        sc, b, beta, r, gam = candidates[idx]
        if not math.isfinite(sc):
            continue

        for outer in range(24):
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break
            improved = False

            for factor in (0.7, 0.85, 1.15, 1.3):
                nb = b * factor
                if nb < 0.02:
                    nb = 0.02
                elif nb > 0.98:
                    nb = 0.98
                s2 = _profile_score_N(n, nb, beta, r, gam, Nscore)
                if math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < sc:
                    sc, b = s2, nb
                    improved = True

            for off in (-0.08, -0.04, 0.04, 0.08):
                nbeta = beta + off
                if nbeta < 0.0:
                    nbeta = 0.0
                elif nbeta > 0.5:
                    nbeta = 0.5
                s2 = _profile_score_N(n, b, nbeta, r, gam, Nscore)
                if math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < sc:
                    sc, beta = s2, nbeta
                    improved = True

            for factor in (0.5, 0.75, 1.33, 2.0):
                nr = r * factor
                if nr < 1e-3:
                    nr = 1e-3
                elif nr > 1e3:
                    nr = 1e3
                s2 = _profile_score_N(n, b, beta, nr, gam, Nscore)
                if math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < sc:
                    sc, r = s2, nr
                    improved = True

            for factor in (0.0, 0.5, 1.5, 3.0):
                ngam = gam * factor
                if ngam < 0.0:
                    ngam = 0.0
                elif ngam > 100.0:
                    ngam = 100.0
                s2 = _profile_score_N(n, b, beta, r, ngam, Nscore)
                if math.isfinite(s2) and s2 < sc:
                    sc, gam = s2, ngam
                    improved = True

            if not improved:
                break

        if sc < candidates[0][0]:
            candidates[0] = (sc, b, beta, r, gam)

    best = candidates[0]
    p = _make_profile(n, best[1], best[2], best[3], best[4])
    if p is None:
        return None, float('inf')
    return p, float(best[0])


def _make_random_fourier(n, amp, rng):
    base = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for k in range(24):
        hi = max(2, n // 2)
        freq = int(rng.integers(1, hi))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        coeff = float(rng.uniform(-amp, amp)) / float(k + 1)
        base += coeff * np.cos((2.0 * np.pi * freq * i) / float(n) + phase)
    p = np.clip(base, 0.0, None)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(p.sum())
    return p / s


def _score_weights_Fp(n, p, N, smooth):
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    cc = np.fft.irfft(Fp * Fp, N)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    c = cc[:L]
    np.maximum(c, c, out=c)
    mx = float(c.max())
    if mx < 0.0:
        mx = 0.0
    score = float(2.0 * n * mx)

    if mx <= 0.0 or smooth <= 0.0:
        return score, c, None, Fp

    scale = float(smooth) / mx
    x = (c - mx) * scale
    np.minimum(x, 0.0, out=x)
    ex = np.exp(x)
    s = float(ex.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return score, c, None, Fp
    w = ex / s
    return score, c, w, Fp


def _h_from_Fp_w(n, w, Fp, N):
    wrev = w[::-1].copy()
    Fw = np.fft.rfft(wrev, N)
    full = np.fft.irfft(Fp * Fw, N)
    h = full[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1].copy()
    np.maximum(h, h, out=h)
    return h


def _local_optimize_mult(p0, n, budget_s, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = int(n)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return p0.copy(), float('inf')
    p /= s

    N = _pow2(3 * n - 2)
    best = p.copy()
    best_score = _score_normalized_N(p, N, n)
    cur_score = best_score

    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s))

    step = 1.0
    smooth = 20.0
    fail = 0
    improve_count = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        score, c, w, Fp = _score_weights_Fp(n, p, N, smooth)
        if w is None:
            break
        cur_score = score

        improved = False
        h = _h_from_Fp_w(n, w, Fp, N)
        y = p * h
        ymean = float(y.mean())
        diff = y - ymean
        yscale = float(np.max(np.abs(diff))) if diff.size else 0.0

        if yscale > 1e-15:
            rel = diff / yscale
            for tr in (step, step * 0.5, step * 0.25, step * 0.125):
                expo = -tr * rel
                np.clip(expo, -3.0, 3.0, out=expo)
                q = p * np.exp(expo)
                q += 1e-12
                qs = float(q.sum())
                if qs <= 0.0:
                    continue
                q /= qs
                ns = _score_normalized_N(q, N, n)
                if math.isfinite(ns) and ns < cur_score - 1e-12:
                    p = q
                    cur_score = ns
                    improved = True
                    if ns < best_score:
                        best = q.copy()
                        best_score = ns
                    step = min(8.0, tr * 1.05)
                    break

        if improved:
            fail = 0
            improve_count += 1
            if improve_count % 120 == 0 and smooth < 500.0:
                smooth *= 1.15
        else:
            fail += 1
            step = max(0.05, step * 0.88)
            if fail % 25 == 0 and smooth > 10.0:
                smooth = max(10.0, smooth * 0.75)

            if fail == 30 or fail == 80 or fail == 150 or fail == 250:
                if fail < 80:
                    sigma = 0.12 + 0.05 * (fail // 10)
                    mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
                    q = p * mult
                    qs = float(q.sum())
                    if qs > 0.0:
                        q /= qs
                else:
                    b = float(rng.uniform(0.10, 0.90))
                    beta = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.5))
                    r = math.exp(float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0)))
                    if rng.random() < 0.3:
                        gam = 0.0
                    else:
                        gam = math.exp(float(rng.uniform(-1.0, 2.5)))
                    p2 = _make_profile(n, b, beta, r, gam)
                    if p2 is None:
                        p2 = p
                    else:
                        s2 = float(p2.sum())
                        if s2 > 0.0:
                            p2 /= s2
                        else:
                            p2 = p
                    mix = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 0.65))
                    q = (1.0 - mix) * p + mix * p2
                    np.maximum(q, q, out=q)
                    qs = float(q.sum())
                    if qs <= 0.0:
                        q = p.copy()
                        qs = 1.0
                    else:
                        q /= qs
                qscore = _score_normalized_N(q, N, n)
                p = q
                cur_score = qscore
                if math.isfinite(qscore) and qscore < best_score:
                    best = q.copy()
                    best_score = qscore
                step = 1.5
                smooth = 20.0
                fail = 0
                improve_count = 0

        it += 1
        if it >= 100000:
            break

    return best, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Generate the best nonnegative coefficient sequence found in time."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    total_budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, total_budget - 45.0)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float('inf')
    best_n = 0

    # Use any provided SOTA construction if it is reasonably sized.
    try:
        hs = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
        hs = None
    except Exception:
        hs = None

    if hs is not None:
        try:
            arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if 128 <= arr.size <= 10000:
                prep = _normalize_seq(arr)
                if prep is not None:
                    sc = _score_seq(prep)
                    if math.isfinite(sc):
                        best_seq = prep
                        best_score = sc
                        best_n = int(prep.size)
                        print(f"Starting from height_sequence_1: score={best_score:.10f}, len={best_n}", flush=True)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Main search over several lengths.
    allocations = ((2048, 320), (4096, 1100))
    for n, alloc in allocations:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 90:
            break

        t0 = time.monotonic()
        param_budget = min(0.30 * alloc, deadline - time.monotonic() - 60.0)
        if param_budget < 5.0:
            continue

        p_param, sc_param = _search_profile(n, param_budget, rng)
        print(f"n={n} profile search score={sc_param:.10f} time={time.monotonic()-t0:.1f}s", flush=True)

        starts = []
        if p_param is not None and math.isfinite(sc_param):
            starts.append((sc_param, p_param))

        p_sqrt = _make_sqrt(n)
        if p_sqrt is not None:
            sc_sqrt = _score_seq(p_sqrt)
            if math.isfinite(sc_sqrt):
                starts.append((sc_sqrt, p_sqrt))

        p_rand = _make_random_fourier(n, 0.65, rng)
        sc_rand = _score_seq(p_rand)
        if math.isfinite(sc_rand):
            starts.append((sc_rand, p_rand))

        starts.sort()
        local_budget = min(alloc - (time.monotonic() - t0), deadline - time.monotonic() - 90.0)
        if local_budget > 10.0 and starts:
            shares = (0.65, 0.25, 0.10)
            for idx in range(min(3, len(starts))):
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 45:
                    break
                sc0, p0 = starts[idx]
                share = local_budget * shares[idx]
                share = min(share, deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0)
                if share <= 2.0:
                    break
                t1 = time.monotonic()
                p2, sc2 = _local_optimize_mult(p0, n, share, rng)
                print(f"n={n} local start {idx} score0={sc0:.10f} -> score={sc2:.10f} time={time.monotonic()-t1:.1f}s", flush=True)
                if math.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
                    best_seq = p2
                    best_score = sc2
                    best_n = int(n)

    # If the initial SOTA sequence was the best, polish it a bit more.
    if best_seq is not None and best_n >= 2:
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 180.0:
            share = min(500.0, rem - 45.0)
            p2, sc2 = _local_optimize_mult(best_seq, best_n, share, rng)
            if math.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
                best_seq = p2
                best_score = sc2
                best_n = int(best_n)
            print(f"Final polish len={best_n} score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)

    if best_seq is None:
        n = 2048
        best_seq = _make_sqrt(n)
        best_n = n

    # Fallback sanity: if something weird happened, use a safe sequence.
    try:
        out = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        out = np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0)
        out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(out.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or out.size == 0:
            out = _make_sqrt(1024)
            s = float(out.sum())
        if s > 0.0:
            out = out / s
    except Exception:
        out = _make_sqrt(1024)

    print(f"Final returned score estimate={best_score:.10f}, length={out.size}", flush=True)
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
